\displaystyle=-\sqrt{3}\,.\phantom{\sqrt{\frac{N^{2}-4}{2}}}

(320)

For the colour representations
D
and
27
, we have given the results for

N=3
. Notice that in all cases except
c_{g\to q\bar{q}}(8)
and
c_{g\to gg}(D)
, the colour non-singlet channel is enhanced over the singlet
one for SU(3).

8
Summary

We have performed a systematic analysis of double parton scattering for
measured transverse momenta, extending the framework for factorisation and
resummation formulated in
Collins:2011zzd
for single parton
scattering with colourless particles in the final state. A major challenge
is the description of soft gluon exchange and the associated rapidity
evolution, which has a much richer colour structure in DPS than in SPS. We
handle this structure by projecting suitable pairs of partons onto definite
colour representations.

Based on perturbative results up to two loops
Vladimirov:2016qkd
, we
have proposed equation (
37
) for the rapidity dependence of the
soft factor in DPS, which is a Collins-Soper equation for matrices in colour
space. Assuming the validity of that equation, we have given a concise
definition of DPDs in (
3.3
) and derived their general
properties. Applied to SPS, our construction provides an alternative form
for the square root factor in the definition of single-parton TMDs by
Collins
Collins:2011zzd
.
Transverse momentum dependent DPDs (DTMDs) follow the evolution equations
(
124
) and (
128
), which can be solved in closed
analytic form as given in (
5.1
). The rapidity evolution of
DTMDs mixes different colour channels, and its solution involves a matrix
exponential in the space of colour representations. One-loop expressions of
the evolution kernels and anomalous dimensions are given in
section
7.2
for all parton combinations and colour channels.

In collinear factorisation, i.e. when the transverse momenta of the
final-state particles are integrated over, the soft factor for DPS simplifies
considerably: it becomes diagonal in colour representation space and
independent of the parton type. This can be shown using only colour algebra
and the fact that pairs of Wilson lines associated with initial and final
state partons cancel as
WW^{\dagger}=\mathbb{1}
. Important consequences of this
result are the simple structure (
4.5
) of the cross section for
DPS processes producing coloured particles, as well as the fact that evolution
in rapidity does not mix different colour
channels for collinear DPDs (called DPDFs here). The corresponding evolution
equations can be found in (
140
) and (
141
), and an
analytic expression that makes the rapidity dependence explicit is provided in
(
5.2
). At one-loop accuracy, the evolution kernels in colour
non-singlet channels are related in a simple way to the usual DGLAP kernels,
as specified in (
7.3.5
).

If the size
q_{T}
of the measured transverse momenta in a DPS process is
large compared with nonperturbative scales
\Lambda
(but still small
compared with the scale
Q
of the hard-scattering processes), one can use
TMD factorisation with DTMDs expressed in terms of perturbative kernels and
simpler hadronic matrix elements. There are two regimes for this
short-distance matching, depending on the relative size of the transverse
distance
\boldsymbol{y}
between the two partons and the distances
\boldsymbol{z}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{z}_{2}
that are conjugate to the measured transverse momenta in
the cross section formula.

In the first regime, when
\boldsymbol{y}
is much larger than
\boldsymbol{z}_{1}
and

\boldsymbol{z}_{2}
, the matching is very similar to the familiar matching of
single parton TMDs onto single parton PDFs. The resulting expressions are
given in (
6.2.2
) and (
6.2.2
). Apart from a
suppression factor for colour non-singlet DPDFs, which is controlled by
their rapidity evolution kernel
{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muJ(\boldsymbol{y})
, we obtain a product
of Sudakov exponentials and of matching kernels (one for each parton) with
DPDFs. At one-loop order, the matching kernels
{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muC(x,\boldsymbol{z})
are
due to ladder-type graphs and coincide with the ones for single-parton TMDs
in the colour singlet channel
R=1
. Most of these kernels have been given
in the literature before, but we have recomputed them in
section
7.3
and extended the calculation to
provide results for all combinations of parton type, polarisation and colour
needed for DPS. We use the HVBM scheme for defining
\gamma_{5}
and the

\epsilon
tensor in dimensional regularisation, but point out a way to
avoid these quantities before taking the number of dimensions to
D=4
.

In the second regime, when
\boldsymbol{y}
is of the same size as
\boldsymbol{z}_{1}

and
\boldsymbol{z}_{2}
, one can match the DTMDs onto collinear distributions of
twist two (i.e. usual PDFs), twist three or twist four. We neglect
collinear twist-three distributions since in an unpolarised proton they are
chiral-odd and hence expected to play a minor role in the regime of small
parton momentum fractions typical of DPS processes. The matching onto
twist-four distributions is presently only known at lowest order, where it
is trivial and given in (
194
). The contribution that matches
onto ordinary PDFs corresponds to the splitting of one parton into two. At
leading order it has the form (
193
) with splitting kernels
given in section
7.4
. An important point is
that in the DPS cross section, terms matching onto collinear distributions
of different twist come with different powers of
\Lambda/q_{T}
and of

\alpha_{s}
as specified in (
195
).

To combine the expressions for matching at large or small
\boldsymbol{y}
, we adapt
the subtraction formalism of
Collins:2011zzd
. In this formalism,
double counting is avoided by a subtraction term in the cross section. This
term can be easily obtained from the small-
\boldsymbol{y}
or the large-
\boldsymbol{y}

expression without further computation, but it requires some care when
choosing scales as discussed in section
6.4
. The same
subtraction formalism is used to combine the cross sections for DPS, SPS and
their interference as specified in sections
5.3
and

6.5
. In the regime where
\Lambda\ll q_{T}\ll Q
one needs
a limited set of nonperturbative quantities for computing the cross section:
the DPDFs
{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muF(x_{i},\boldsymbol{y})
and their rapidity evolution kernel

{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muJ(\boldsymbol{y})
, collinear twist-four distributions (which arise from
DPDs in the short-distance limit) and ordinary single-parton PDFs. The
colour octet kernel
{}^{8}\mskip-1.5muJ(\boldsymbol{y})
turns out to be equal to the
Collins-Soper kernel for single-gluon TMDs, providing a surprising connection
between two rather different areas of factorisation.

In the multi-scale regime
\Lambda\ll q_{T}\ll Q
we thus have a
significantly increased predictive power of the theory. Rather than a huge set
of transverse-momentum dependent distributions, the TMD cross section involves
the same nonperturbative functions as the cross section integrated over
q_{T}
.
All other ingredients in the factorisation formula are of perturbative
nature. In most (although not all) cases they are known at least to the
first nontrivial order in
\alpha_{s}
. The results presented in this work
provide a starting point for phenomenological investigations, for instance of
electroweak diboson production with measured transverse boson momenta. An
important task will be to assess which types of correlations and which regions
of
\boldsymbol{y}
are important in a given process and kinematical setting.

Before concluding, we wish to discuss the question of scheme dependence
in the treatment of large gluon rapidities. Matrix elements of Wilson lines
along lightlike directions typically have rapidity divergences, which
originate from regions of gluon momenta with infinite rapidity. In the
present paper, we follow the approach of Collins
Collins:2011zzd
and
use Wilson line directions away from the light cone to regulate these
divergences. The regulated Wilson lines then depend on rapidity variables.
In the DPD construction laid out in section
3.3
, the
parameter
\zeta
is defined in terms of a central rapidity
Y_{C}
that
specifies the range of gluon rapidities effectively included in the DPD.
Several other schemes for regulating rapidity divergences have been proposed
and used in the literature, see

GarciaEchevarria:2011rb
;
Echevarria:2015byo
;
Echevarria:2016scs
;
Chiu:2011qc
;
Chiu:2012ir
;
Li:2016axz
and
Becher:2010tm
. Quantities that
depend on a regulator variable will in general differ between schemes. By
contrast, quantities that enter a cross section formula in the same way in two
schemes must be equal. This holds in particular for TMDs, and hence also for
the associated Collins-Soper kernels and matching coefficients onto PDFs.
Discussions of equivalence between specific schemes can be found in

Collins:2012uy
;
Echevarria:2012js
;
Collins:2017oxh
.

It follows that our results in sections
4
to

7
are independent of the rapidity regulator
scheme.
16
16

16

A few equations in these sections are obviously specific to
the regulator we use, such as the definition (
125
) of
\zeta
in
terms of
Y_{C}
.

Of course, this only holds if one defines the variables
\zeta
and

\bar{\zeta}
in a consistent way across different schemes, see for instance
appendix
B
here and section 6.1 in

Vladimirov:2017ksc
. We have checked by explicit calculation that the
one-loop matching coefficients presented in
section
7.3
remain the same when one uses the

\delta
regulator specified in
GarciaEchevarria:2011rb
. By contrast,
our arguments in section
3
are specifically formulated
for Wilson lines with finite rapidities and would need to be adapted to other
schemes, using an appropriate translation between the regulator variables as
given in appendix
B
for a particular case.

As already noted, we absorb the soft factor into the DPDs, corresponding
to what is done for SPS in several TMD factorisation approaches

Collins:2011zzd
;
GarciaEchevarria:2011rb
. A different route was taken in

Manohar:2012jr
, where the soft factor explicitly appears in the final
DPS cross section formula. Such an approach is often taken in the SCET
literature in situations when the soft factor can be computed in perturbation
theory (e.g. for single Drell-Yan production at large
q_{T}
). In DPS,
however, one needs the soft factor at large distances
\boldsymbol{y}
. Including
it into the DPDs therefore limits the number of nonperturbative functions
required to compute the cross section.

Given the results of the present paper and the arguments regarding Glauber
gluon exchange in
Diehl:2015bca
, we consider that TMD factorisation for
DPS is now established at the same level of rigour as for single hard
scattering, apart from the following issues.
(i)
: As already
mentioned, there is no complete all-order proof of the properties we assumed
for the soft factor in section
3.2
, but significant
process in this direction has been made in
Vladimirov:2017ksc
.

(ii)
: There is no all-order proof of the nonabelian Ward identities required
for factorising soft gluon exchange between left and right moving partons into
the soft matrix.
(iii)
: As noted below (
253
), we have
omitted Wilson line self interactions in our calculation, following the logic
discussed for SPS in chapter 13.7 of
Collins:2011zzd
. A more thorough
understanding of this issue, in particular for DPS, has not yet been achieved.
Progress on any of these three points will constitute a further step towards a
rigorous treatment of double parton scattering in QCD.

.

;

.

Appendix A
Matrix manipulations for the soft factor

Referring to section
3.2
, we now show that the set of
properties 1a – 1c for the soft factor is equivalent with properties 2a
– 2b. As a corollary, we find that the soft matrix
S(Y)
is positive
definite for arbitrary large
Y
if these properties hold.

Properties 2a – 2b follow from properties 1a – 1c.

The matrix
s(0)
is nonsingular by assumption (property 1b), so that one
can define a matrix
\widehat{K}
by

\displaystyle\widehat{K}s(0)

\displaystyle=s(0)K\,.

(321)

The differential equation (
35
) (property 1a) is thus solved by

\displaystyle s(Y)

\displaystyle=s(0)\mskip 1.5mue^{YK}=e^{Y\widehat{K}}\mskip 1.5mus(0)

(322)

for all
Y
. We now define

\displaystyle\widehat{S}(Y)

\displaystyle=s(Y/2)\,s^{\dagger}(Y/2)\,.

(323)

Using (
36
) (property 1c) for
Y_{0}=Y/2
, we see that

\widehat{S}(Y)
approximates
S(Y)
for
Y\gg 1
.

For any complex vector
a
one has

\displaystyle a^{\dagger}\,\widehat{S}(Y)\mskip 1.5mua

\displaystyle=b^{\dagger}\mskip 1.5mub\,,

(324)

with
b=s^{\dagger}(Y/2)\,a
. The expression in (
324
) is
obviously positive or zero, and since
s^{\dagger}(Y/2)
is nonsingular by
assumption (property 1b), it is zero only for
a=0
. Therefore

\widehat{S}(Y)
is positive definite for all
Y
. This implies property
2b, and it also implies that
S(Y)
is positive for all
Y
where it can
be approximated by
\widehat{S}(Y)
.

Multiplying (
321
) with
s^{\dagger}(0)
on the right, and using
that
K
is Hermitian, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{K}\widehat{S}(0)=\widehat{S}(0)\widehat{K}^{\dagger}\,.

(325)

We thus finally have

\displaystyle\widehat{S}(Y)

\displaystyle=e^{Y\widehat{K}/2}\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{S}(0)e^{Y\widehat{K}^{\dagger}/2}=e^{Y\widehat{K}}\widehat{S}(0)\,,

(326)

where we used (
322
) and (
323
) in the first step
and (
325
) in the second one. This implies property 2a for
Y\gg 1
, where
\widehat{S}(Y)
approximates
S(Y)
.

Properties 1a – 1c follow from properties 2a – 2b.

We start by defining the matrix
\widehat{S}(Y)
for all
Y
by

\displaystyle\widehat{S}(Y)

\displaystyle=\lim_{Y_{0}\to\infty}e^{(Y-Y_{0})\widehat{K}}S(Y_{0})\,.

(327)

This obviously satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial Y}\,\widehat{S}(Y)

\displaystyle=\widehat{K}\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{S}(Y)

(328)

exactly. For
Y,Y_{0}\gg 1
one has
S(Y)=e^{(Y-Y_{0})\widehat{K}}S(Y_{0})
by virtue of (
37
) (property 2a), which implies

\widehat{S}(Y)=S(Y)
for
Y\gg 1
.

Because
S(Y)
is Hermitian according to (
3
), it follows
from (
37
) that
\widehat{K}S(Y)=S(Y)\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{K}^{\dagger}
for
Y\gg 1
. Using this, one deduces from
(
327
) that
\widehat{S}(Y)
is Hermitian as well. With
(
328
) we then have

\displaystyle\widehat{K}\widehat{S}(Y)=\widehat{S}(Y)\widehat{K}^{\dagger}

(329)

for all
Y
. The solution of (
328
) can thus be written as

\displaystyle\widehat{S}(Y)

\displaystyle=e^{(Y-Y_{1})\widehat{K}}\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{S}(Y_{1})=e^{(Y-Y_{1}-Y_{0})\widehat{K}}\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{S}(Y_{1})\mskip 1.5mue^{Y_{0}\widehat{K}^{\dagger}}

(330)

for arbitrary
Y_{0}
and
Y_{1}
.

We now recall the definition of the square root
{M}^{1/2}
of a positive
definite matrix
M
. One can write
M=V^{\dagger}DV
, where
V
is
unitary and
D
is diagonal with positive entries
d_{ii}
. One defines

{D}^{1/2}
as the diagonal matrix with entries
\sqrt{d_{ii}}
, and
furthermore
\sqrt{M}=V^{\dagger}{D}^{1/2}V
. If
M
is real then
V

can be taken as an orthogonal matrix, so that
{M}^{1/2}
is real as well.
Since
\widehat{S}(Y_{1})
is positive definite for some
Y_{1}
(property
2b), one can use (
330
) with
Y_{0}=(Y-Y_{1})/2
and write

\widehat{S}(Y)=t(Y)\,t^{\dagger}(Y)
, where
t(Y)=e^{(Y-Y_{1})\widehat{K}/2}\mskip 1.5mu\bigl[\widehat{S}(Y_{1})\bigr]{}^{1/2}
is a
nonsingular matrix. With the same argument as given below
(
323
), it then follows that
\widehat{S}(Y)
is positive for
all
Y
.

We can now define

\displaystyle s(Y)

\displaystyle=e^{Y\widehat{K}}\mskip 1.5mu\bigl[\widehat{S}(0)\bigr]{}^{1/2}\mskip 1.5muU\,,

(331)

where
U
is a unitary matrix.
s(Y)
is nonsingular (property 1b) and
satisfies the decomposition (
36
) (property 1c) by virtue of
(
330
) with
Y_{1}=0
. Defining

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle=U^{\dagger}\mskip 1.5mu\bigl[\widehat{S}(0)\bigr]{}^{-1/2}\widehat{K}\,\bigl[\widehat{S}(0)\bigr]{}^{1/2}\mskip 1.5muU\,,

(332)

one readily finds that
K^{\dagger}=K
and

\displaystyle s(Y)

\displaystyle=\bigl[\widehat{S}(0)\bigr]{}^{1/2}\mskip 1.5muU\mskip 1.5mue^{YK}\,,

(333)

which implies (
35
) (property 1a). The definitions
(
331
) and (
332
) are consistent with (
321
)
above. We note that a more general definition of
s(Y)
and
K
is
obtained by replacing
\widehat{S}(0)\to\widehat{S}(Y_{1})
in
(
331
) to (
333
), as well as
Y\to(Y-Y_{1}/2)
in the
exponentials there.

Restrictions on
U
.

In principle, the matrix
U
can be chosen freely as a smooth function of

\boldsymbol{z}_{1}
,
\boldsymbol{z}_{2}
and
\boldsymbol{y}
, but independent of
Y
and of
the renormalisation scale
\mu
. We restrict ourselves to choices such that

U=1
for
\boldsymbol{y}=\boldsymbol{0}
. Since
S(Y)
is diagonal in that case (see
section
4.4
), the same then holds for
\widehat{S}(Y)
,

s(Y)
and for
K=\widehat{K}
.

We furthermore impose a number of restrictions on
U
in different partonic
channels. We thus demand that

\displaystyle U_{qq}=

\displaystyle U_{\bar{q}\bar{q}}^{*}\,,

\displaystyle U_{q\bar{q}}

\displaystyle=U_{\bar{q}q}^{*}\,,

\displaystyle U_{qg}

\displaystyle=U_{\bar{q}g}^{*}\,,

\displaystyle U_{gg}

\displaystyle=U_{gg}^{*}\,,

(334)

which is necessary to obtain the corresponding relations (
41
) for

s_{a_{1}a_{2}}
from those for
S_{a_{1}a_{2}}
. As discussed in
section
4.3
, we also require that
{}^{RR^{\prime}}\mskip-1.5muU
, defined in
analogy to (
81
), is a real valued matrix in representation space
and satisfies

\displaystyle{}^{RR^{\prime}}\mskip-1.5muU_{qq}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1},\boldsymbol{z}_{2},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle={}^{RR^{\prime}}\mskip-1.5muU_{q\bar{q}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1},-\boldsymbol{z}_{2},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle={}^{RR^{\prime}}\mskip-1.5muU_{\bar{q}q}(-\boldsymbol{z}_{1},\boldsymbol{z}_{2},\boldsymbol{y})={}^{RR^{\prime}}\mskip-1.5muU_{\bar{q}\bar{q}}(-\boldsymbol{z}_{1},-\boldsymbol{z}_{2},\boldsymbol{y})\,.

(335)

We note that the trivial choice
U_{a_{1}a_{2}}=\mathbb{1}
satisfies all
requirements just listed and gives
{}^{RR^{\prime}}\mskip-1.5muU_{a_{1}a_{2}}=\delta_{RR^{\prime}}
.

Appendix B
Comparison with the
\delta
regulator
scheme

As emphasised in section
8
, our treatment of soft and
collinear factors uses Wilson line directions away from the light cone to
regulate rapidity divergences, as was done by Collins in

Collins:2011zzd
. The regulated Wilson lines then depend on large but
finite rapidities.

The two-loop calculation of the DPS soft factor in

Vladimirov:2016qkd
was performed using the
\delta
regulator scheme
specified in
Echevarria:2015byo
;
Echevarria:2016scs
. Given the
particular relevance of this calculation for our assumptions on the soft
factor in section
3.2
, we now compare the variables in that
scheme with the ones in the scheme of Collins. For simplicity we perform the
comparison for the SPS soft factor. It is easy to extend the following
arguments to DPS, including the appropriate rescaling of
\zeta
, see
(
61
) and (
125
). We note that there are two earlier
variants of the
\delta
regulator, described in

GarciaEchevarria:2011rb
and
Echevarria:2012js
, and compared with
the Collins scheme in
Collins:2012uy
and
Echevarria:2012js
,
respectively.

In the
\delta
regulator scheme of

Echevarria:2015byo
;
Echevarria:2016scs
, Wilson lines are taken along
lightlike paths but modified by an exponential damping at large distances

z^{-}
(
z^{+}
). This damping is controlled by a parameter
\delta^{+}

(
\delta^{-}
), which transforms like the plus (minus) component of a vector
under longitudinal boosts. One has a correspondence of variables

\displaystyle Y_{R}

\displaystyle\,\leftrightarrow\,\log\frac{\mu}{\delta^{-}}\,,

\displaystyle Y_{L}

\displaystyle\,\leftrightarrow\,\log\frac{\delta^{+}}{\mu}\,.

(336)

Due to boost invariance, the soft factor
S
only depends on
Y_{R}-Y_{L}\leftrightarrow-\log(\delta^{+}\delta^{-}/\mu^{2})
. The correspondence
indicated by
\leftrightarrow
is valid when taking derivatives, so that

\mu
in (
336
) could be replaced by another quantity that has
dimension of mass and is boost invariant. In our formalism, the soft
factor can be split into

\displaystyle S(Y_{R}-Y_{L})

\displaystyle=\sqrt{S(2Y_{R}-2Y_{C})}\,\sqrt{S(2Y_{C}-2Y_{L})}\,,

(337)

and the same splitting is performed with the
\delta
regulator, with
the following correspondence of variables:

\displaystyle 2(Y_{R}-Y_{C})

\displaystyle\,\leftrightarrow\,\log\frac{\mu^{2}}{(\delta^{-})^{2}}\,\frac{2(\bar{k}^{-})^{2}}{\bar{\zeta}\rule{0.0pt}{9.47217pt}}\,,

\displaystyle 2(Y_{C}-Y_{L})

\displaystyle\,\leftrightarrow\,\log\frac{\mu^{2}}{(\delta^{+})^{2}}\,\frac{2(k^{+})^{2}}{\zeta}\,.

(338)

Here the momentum components
k^{+}
and
\bar{k}^{-}
correspond to the two
partons initiating a hard scattering, and we have normalised the rapidity
parameters as
\zeta\bar{\zeta}=(2k^{+}\bar{k}^{-})^{2}
, in accordance to
the convention (
61
) used in the present paper.
17
17

17

The
normalisation of
\zeta
and
\bar{\zeta}
is not relevant for the Collins
Soper equation, but it does matter for the argument of the anomalous
dimensions
\gamma_{F,a}
.

On both sides of (
338
), the two expressions add up to twice
the argument of the original soft factor in the relevant formalism.
After the square roots of the soft factor are combined with unsubtracted
collinear matrix elements (see (
60
)), one removes the
rapidity regulator in either scheme, taking
\delta^{-}\to 0
and
\delta^{+}\to 0
or
Y_{R}\to\infty
and
Y_{L}\to-\infty
. The resulting
distributions depend on the rapidity variable
\zeta
or
\bar{\zeta}
.

Appendix C
Combining SPS and DPS for different hard scales

In this appendix, we discuss the generalisation of the scale setting discussed
in section
6.5
to the case where the scales of the two DPS
processes (
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
) and of the SPS process (
\mu_{h}
) are
different. In the four contributions (
6.5
) to the
cross section, we now take

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{SPS}}:

\displaystyle f(x_{1}+x_{2},\boldsymbol{Z})

\displaystyle\sim E_{2}(\mu_{h};\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{DPS/SPS}}:

\displaystyle D(x_{i},\boldsymbol{y}_{+},\boldsymbol{Z})

\displaystyle\sim E_{3}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}|\mu_{h};\mu_{Z})\,U(\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{SPS/DPS}}:

\displaystyle D(x_{i},\boldsymbol{y}_{-},\boldsymbol{Z})

\displaystyle\sim E_{3}(\mu_{h}|\mu_{1},\mu_{2};\mu_{Z})\,U^{*}(\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{DPS}}:

\displaystyle F(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle\sim E_{4}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}|\mu_{1},\mu_{2};\mu_{Z})\,U(\mu_{Z})\,U^{*}(\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})+F_{\text{int}}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})\,.

(339)

In
E_{3}
and
E_{4}
, we now allow different scales for all three or four
parton legs (see the remarks at the ends of sections
3.4

and
5.3
). Scale arguments in the amplitude and in its
complex conjugate are separated by a vertical bar “
|
”. If only a single
scale is given, such as
\mu_{Z}
in (
C
), then all scales
are taken equal. In the subtraction terms for the SPS/DPS interference, we
now take

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{DPS/SPS},y_{+}\to 0}:D

\displaystyle\sim E_{3}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2}|\mu_{h};\hat{\mu}_{h})\,U(\hat{\mu}_{h})\,E_{2}(\hat{\mu}_{h};\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})

\displaystyle\qquad\mbox{with\penalty\ }\hat{\mu}_{h}=p(\nu\mskip 1.5mu|\boldsymbol{y}_{+}|;\mu_{Z},\mu_{h})\mbox{ \penalty\ and\penalty\ }\bar{\mu}_{i}=p(\nu\mskip 1.5mu|\boldsymbol{y}_{+}|;\mu_{i},\mu_{h})\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{SPS/DPS},y_{-}\to 0}:D

\displaystyle\sim E_{3}(\mu_{h}|\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2};\hat{\mu}_{h})\,U^{*}(\hat{\mu}_{h})\,E_{2}(\hat{\mu}_{h};\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})

\displaystyle\qquad\mbox{with\penalty\ }\hat{\mu}_{h}=p(\nu\mskip 1.5mu|\boldsymbol{y}_{-}|;\mu_{Z},\mu_{h})\mbox{ \penalty\ and\penalty\ }\bar{\mu}_{i}=p(\nu\mskip 1.5mu|\boldsymbol{y}_{-}|;\mu_{i},\mu_{h})

(340)

instead of (
6.5
).
The profile scales
\hat{\mu}
are the same as in the single-scale case of
section
6.5
, whilst
\bar{\mu}
interpolates between the
different hard scales. The relation (
215
) between the
hard-scattering amplitudes for SPS and DPS holds for equal scales of

\alpha_{s}
in all its terms. In all subtraction terms, the scale of

\alpha_{s}
in the DPS amplitudes
H_{\alpha_{1}\beta_{1}}
and
H_{\alpha_{2}\beta_{2}}
should be taken as
\bar{\mu}_{i}
. This is for instance relevant
for
gg\to H
, which involves the strong coupling already at leading order.
With the choices just specified, one finds that the limiting relation
(
214
) for the subtraction term still holds.

Generalising (
6.5
), the subtraction terms for DPS
can be taken as follows:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{DPS},y_{-}\to 0}:

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\sim E_{4}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2}|\mu_{1},\mu_{2};\hat{\mu}_{1},\hat{\mu}_{2}|\hat{\mu}_{h},\hat{\mu}_{h})\,U^{*}(\hat{\mu}_{h})\,E_{3}(\hat{\mu}_{1},\hat{\mu}_{2}|\hat{\mu}_{h};\mu_{Z})\,U(\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{DPS},y_{+}\to 0}:

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\sim E_{4}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}|\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2};\hat{\mu}_{h},\hat{\mu}_{h}|\hat{\mu}_{1},\hat{\mu}_{2})\,U(\hat{\mu}_{h})\,E_{3}(\hat{\mu}_{h}|\hat{\mu}_{1},\hat{\mu}_{2};\mu_{Z})\,U^{*}(\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})\,,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{DPS},y_{\pm}\to 0}:

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\sim E_{4}(\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2}|\bar{\mu}_{1},\bar{\mu}_{2};\hat{\mu}_{h})\,U(\hat{\mu}_{h})\ U^{*}(\hat{\mu}_{h})\,E_{2}(\hat{\mu}_{h};\mu_{Z})\,f(\mu_{Z})

(341)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}

\displaystyle=p\bigl(\nu\min\{|\boldsymbol{y}_{+}|,|\boldsymbol{y}_{-}|\};\mu_{Z},\mu_{h}\bigr)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}_{i}

\displaystyle=p\bigl(\nu\min\{|\boldsymbol{y}_{+}|,|\boldsymbol{y}_{-}|\};\mu_{i},\mu_{h}\bigr)\,.

(342)

For
|\boldsymbol{y}_{+}|\gg 1/\nu
and
|\boldsymbol{y}_{-}|\gg 1/\nu
one finds the
limiting behaviour in (
218
), whereas all three terms in
(
C
) tend to the 1v1 contribution to DPS if one or
both of the transverse distances is of order
1/\nu
. Overall, we thus find
that the subtraction formalism can be adapted to work in the multi-scale case.

Appendix D
Feynman rules

In this appendix we give the Feynman rules used in our calculation of the
matching coefficients in section
7.3
. We
compute cut graphs and thus need in particular the rules for propagators and
vertices to the right of the cut (corresponding to the complex conjugate
amplitude). This requires special care for the three-gluon vertex. A
derivation of these rules can for instance be found in

Belitsky:1997ay
for the QCD Lagrangian (but not for eikonal lines).
The rules given here correspond to the conventions in the chapters 3 and 7
of
Collins:2011zzd
. In particular, the coupling constant is defined
such that the covariant derivative reads
D_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}+igA^{a}{}_{\!\!\!\mu}\,t^{a}
.

The Feynman rules for the propagators and vertices arising from the QCD
Lagrangian in Feynman gauge can be found in
figure
12
, where we use the notation

\displaystyle V^{\mu\nu\rho}(p,q,r)

\displaystyle=(p-q)^{\rho}g^{\mu\nu}+(q-r)^{\mu}g^{\nu\rho}+(r-p)^{\nu}g^{\rho\mu}\,.

(343)

The four-gluon vertex is not given here since it does not appear in our
calculations.

Figure 12:
Feynman rules for propagators and
vertices in cut graphs. We use Feynman gauge and set the quark mass to zero.

V^{\mu\nu\rho}(p,q,r)
is defined in (
343
). With these
rules for cut quark and antiquark lines, there is no minus sign for a
closed fermion loop going across the cut.

Figure 13:
Feynman rules involving eikonal lines
along the direction
v
. The colour indices
r
and
s
refer to either
the fundamental or the adjoint representation, whereas
j
and
k
are
colour triplet and
a
,
b
and
c
are colour octet indices.

Figure 14:
Feynman rule corresponding to the
operator in a gluon distribution when an eikonal line along
n
is
attached to the gluon. The Lorentz index
j
is transverse w.r.t. the
lightlike direction
n
. The rule for the graph without the eikonal line
is obtained by setting
p=q
.

The Feynman rules for the propagators and couplings involving eikonal lines
are given in figure
14
. They arise from the
expansion of Wilson line operators, given by (
16
) and its analogue
for the adjoint representation. We use there the notation of

Diehl:2011yj
, where open and closed circles at the ends of eikonal
lines (in addition to arrows on and above them) were introduced as a way to
make the correspondence between graphs and mathematical expressions unique.
Let us briefly explain this.

First of all, the full circle indicates the (relative) past and the open
circle the (relative) future time direction when considering the path of the
Wilson line in space-time. This determines the sign of
i0
in the eikonal
propagators. Secondly, the arrow
above
the eikonal line propagator
corresponds to the momentum flow, just as for quark and gluon propagators,
and is necessary because the eikonal propagator is linear in the momentum
carried by the line. Finally, the arrow
on
the eikonal line fixes
the overall sign of the propagator and determines the order of contraction
of colour indices, with matrix multiplication going against the direction of
the arrow. This is the same convention as for Dirac indices on fermion
lines. For eikonal lines in the fundamental colour representation, this
arrow indicates the canonical order of multiplication, with expressions
involving Gell-Mann matrices
t_{a}
rather than their transpose

(t_{a})^{T}
. For adjoint eikonal lines, the order in which the indices are
arranged can be chosen freely (writing e.g.
f_{abc}
or
-f_{acb}
).

The vertices for a gluon coupling to a fundamental or an adjoint eikonal line
are given in the third and fourth line of
figure
14
, respectively. In the last row, the
relative sign between the vertices to the left and the right of the cut can
be understood from the relation
f_{cab}=-i(T^{a})_{cb}
, where
T^{a}
acts
as a generator of the colour group. The arrow on the line is required to
distinguish the role of the indices
c
and
b
, given that
f_{cab}
is
antisymmetric in its indices.
18
18

18

We note that in figure 7.10 of

Collins:2011zzd
a minus sign
is missing in the coupling of a gluon to an adjoint eikonal line on the
right of the cut. Otherwise, we agree with the Feynman rules given
there.

In figure
14
we give the Feynman rule for the
operator in a gluon distribution when an eikonal line is attached to the
gluon. This rule was derived in section 7.6 of
Collins:2011zzd
, see
also
Collins:1981uw
;
Collins:2008sg
. It can be obtained by expanding
the operator
n_{\mu}\mskip 1.5muF^{\mu j}(z)\,W(z,n)
in
g
. The Lorentz index
j

in the gluon field strength is restricted to be transverse to the lightlike
vector
n
. Note that this rule corresponds to the gluon operator
(
17
) only if the Wilson line is along
v_{L}=n
.

Appendix E
Useful integrals

In this appendix, we give a number of Fourier integrals that are useful for
the calculations in section
7.3
. We start with

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2-2\epsilon}\boldsymbol{k}}{\left(\boldsymbol{k}^{2}\right)^{\alpha}}\,e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{z}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}{4\pi}\right)^{\epsilon+\alpha-1}\frac{\pi^{\alpha}\mskip 1.5mu\Gamma(1-\epsilon-\alpha)}{\Gamma(\alpha)},

(344)

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2-2\epsilon}\boldsymbol{k}}{\left(\boldsymbol{k}^{2}\right)^{\alpha}}\,e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{z}}\,\log(\boldsymbol{k}^{2})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}{4\pi}\right)^{\epsilon+\alpha-1}\frac{\pi^{\alpha}\mskip 1.5mu\Gamma(1-\epsilon-\alpha)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\biggl[\mskip 1.5mu\log\frac{4}{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}+\psi(\alpha)+\psi(1-\alpha-\epsilon)\mskip 1.5mu\biggr]\,,

(345)

where
\psi(x)=\frac{d}{dz}\log\Gamma(z)
is the digamma function. One
can obtain (
345
) by differentiation of
(
344
) with respect to
\alpha
. A useful relation for
integrals involving tensors is given by

\displaystyle\int\,\frac{d^{2-2\epsilon}{\boldsymbol{k}}}{\left(\boldsymbol{k}^{2}\right)^{\alpha}}\,e^{i{\boldsymbol{k}}\boldsymbol{z}}\,\boldsymbol{k}^{j}\boldsymbol{k}^{j^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi^{\alpha-1}\,\Gamma(3-\alpha-\epsilon)}{\Gamma(\alpha)}\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}{4\pi}\right)^{\epsilon+\alpha-2}\biggl[\frac{\boldsymbol{z}^{j}\boldsymbol{z}^{j^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}+\frac{\delta^{jj^{\prime}}}{2(\epsilon+\alpha-2)}\biggr]\,.

(346)

We obtain (
346
) by rewriting
\boldsymbol{k}^{j}\boldsymbol{k}^{j^{\prime}}
as
-\partial^{2}/(\partial\boldsymbol{z}^{j}\partial\boldsymbol{z}^{j^{\prime}})
on the l.h.s. of (
346
) and then
performing these derivatives on the r.h.s. of (
344
). From the
above equations, it follows that

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{2-2\epsilon}\boldsymbol{k}}{\boldsymbol{k}^{2}}\,e^{i\boldsymbol{k}\boldsymbol{z}}\,\biggl[\frac{k^{jj^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{k}^{2}}+f(\epsilon)\,\delta^{jj^{\prime}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=-\pi\Gamma(1-\epsilon)\left(\frac{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}{4\pi}\right)^{\epsilon}\biggl[\frac{z^{jj^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{z}^{2}}+\frac{f(\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\,\delta^{jj^{\prime}}\biggr]\,,

(347)

where the traceless symmetric tensors
k^{jj^{\prime}}
and
z^{jj^{\prime}}
are defined by
(
256
) and
f(\epsilon)
is an arbitrary function. The integral
over
k^{jj^{\prime}}/\boldsymbol{k}^{4}
is thus finite, whereas the one over
f(\epsilon)/\boldsymbol{k}^{2}
has an ultraviolet pole if
f(\epsilon)
is finite at
\epsilon=0
.

Appendix F
Tensor decomposition of DPDs

In this appendix, we give the tensor decompositions of DTMDs and DPDFs in
terms of scalar and pseudoscalar functions. We emphasise that these
decompositions are only meant to be complete in the
2
physical transverse
dimensions. In calculations that have to be done in
2-2\epsilon

transverse dimensions, one must either avoid the decompositions below (as we
do) or extend them appropriately.

DTMDs depend on the three transverse vectors
\boldsymbol{y}
,
\boldsymbol{z}_{1}
and

\boldsymbol{z}_{2}
, but one can construct the tensors necessary for their
decomposition from
\boldsymbol{y}
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}=\epsilon^{jj^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{y}^{j^{\prime}}

(348)

together with the invariants
\delta^{jj^{\prime}}
and
\epsilon^{jj^{\prime}}
. This is
because in
2 dimensions there are only two linearly independent vectors. The choice of
the above two vectors results in the most straightforward correspondence
between the DTMDs and the DPDFs, where only the vectors
\boldsymbol{y}
and
\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}

can be used. The decomposition of the double quark distributions

Diehl:2011yj
reads

\displaystyle F_{qq}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=f_{qq}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})\,,

\displaystyle F_{\Delta q\Delta q}

\displaystyle=f_{\Delta q\Delta q}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\Delta q\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=g_{\Delta q\mskip 1.5muq}\,,

\displaystyle F_{q\Delta q}

\displaystyle=g_{q\Delta q}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\delta q\mskip 1.5muq}^{j}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}Mf_{\delta q\mskip 1.5muq}+\boldsymbol{y}^{j}Mg_{\delta q\mskip 1.5muq}\,,

\displaystyle F_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{j}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}Mf_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+\boldsymbol{y}^{j}Mg_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{jj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{jj^{\prime}}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+2\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}M^{2}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t}

\displaystyle\quad+2\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}M^{2}g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{s}+(\boldsymbol{y}^{j}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j^{\prime}}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}\boldsymbol{y}^{j^{\prime}})\,M^{2}g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{a}\,,

(349)

where
M
is the proton mass and the tensor
\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,kk^{\prime}}
is defined in
(
19
). Note that one has

\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,kk^{\prime}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,ll^{\prime}}=\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,ll^{\prime}}
. We employ a shorthand
notation where vectors
\boldsymbol{y}
or
\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}
appearing as an index of
\tau

denote contraction, i.e.
\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}=\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,kk^{\prime}}\,\boldsymbol{y}^{k}\boldsymbol{y}^{k^{\prime}}
etc.
f
and
g
denote
scalar and pseudo-scalar functions respectively. The pseudoscalar functions
are absent in the decomposition of DPDFs because one cannot construct a
pseudoscalar from
\boldsymbol{y}
alone. Decompositions analogous to
(
F
) hold for quark-antiquark distributions and for
distributions of two antiquarks.

For quark-gluon distributions we write

\displaystyle F_{qg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=f_{qg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})\,,

\displaystyle F_{\Delta q\Delta g}

\displaystyle=f_{\Delta q\Delta g}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\Delta q\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=g_{\Delta q\mskip 1.5mug}\,,

\displaystyle F_{q\Delta g}

\displaystyle=g_{q\Delta g}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mug}^{j}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}Mf_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mug}+\boldsymbol{y}^{j}Mg_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mug}\,,

\displaystyle F_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{jj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}M^{2}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}+\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}M^{2}g_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{j,kk^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=-\tau^{\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}j,kk^{\prime}}Mf_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-(\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}+\boldsymbol{y}^{j}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}})\,M^{3}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}

\displaystyle\quad-\tau^{\boldsymbol{y}j,kk^{\prime}}Mg_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-(\boldsymbol{y}^{j}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}-\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}^{j}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}})\,M^{3}g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}\,.

(350)

Analogous decompositions hold when the quark is replaced by an antiquark.
Two-gluon distributions are decomposed as

\displaystyle F_{gg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=f_{gg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})\,,

\displaystyle F_{\Delta g\Delta g}

\displaystyle=f_{\Delta g\Delta g}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\Delta g\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=g_{\Delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\,,

\displaystyle F_{g\Delta g}

\displaystyle=g_{g\Delta g}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}^{jj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}+\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}M^{2}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\,,

\displaystyle F_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{jj^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}M^{2}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}+\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}M^{2}g_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{jj^{\prime},kk^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,kk^{\prime}}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}/2+(\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}-\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}})\,M^{4}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}

\displaystyle\quad+(\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}+\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}})\,M^{4}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{s}

\displaystyle\quad+(\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}-\tau^{jj^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y}}\tau^{kk^{\prime}\!,\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}})\,M^{4}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{a}\,.

(351)

We note that in the decomposition of each DTMD, the tensors multiplying
different terms are orthogonal to each other.

The decompositions for the nonzero DPDFs in terms of real-valued scalar
functions have already been given in
Diehl:2011yj
;
Diehl:2013mla
. They
can be directly obtained from the DTMD decompositions above by setting all
pseudo-scalar functions (i.e. all
g
’s) equal to zero (and removing the
arguments
\boldsymbol{z}_{i}
in
F
and
f
).

Appendix G
Matching coefficients for (pseudo)scalar DPDs

Using the decompositions in the previous subsection and the results for the
one-loop matching coefficients given in section
7.3.4
, we can
perform the necessary tensor contractions and obtain a full set of matching
equations at the level of scalar and pseudoscalar distributions. We emphasise
that this is done in the two physical space-time dimensions, since all
intermediate calculations requiring dimensional regularisation have been
completed at this point.
For definiteness, let us recall that the tensor-valued coefficients in
section
7.3.4
appear in the matching relations as

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muF^{jj^{\prime}\!,kk^{\prime}}_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y};\zeta)

\displaystyle={}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muC^{jj^{\prime}\!,ll^{\prime}}_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{1}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{z}_{1};x_{1}\zeta/x_{2})\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muC^{kk^{\prime}\!,mm^{\prime}}_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{2}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{z}_{2};x_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\zeta/x_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\underset{x_{2}}{\otimes}{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muF^{ll^{\prime}\!,mm^{\prime}}_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{i}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{y};\zeta)+\{\text{quark-gluon mixing terms}\}

(352)

for the distribution
F_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}
and in an analogous manner in
other relations involving DPDs that carry polarisation indices. In an
expansion up to
\mathcal{O}(\alpha_{s})
, one keeps the one-loop terms only in
one of the two matching coefficients.

In the following we give the terms with matching coefficients for the parton
with momentum fraction
x_{1}
; the terms for the parton with momentum fraction

x_{2}
are fully analogous. We omit colour labels
R
for brevity. With the
tensor structure of the kernels in section
7.3.4
we then
obtain for two-quark distributions

\displaystyle f_{qq}

\displaystyle=C_{qq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qq}+C_{qg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gq}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\Delta q\Delta q}

\displaystyle=C_{\Delta q\Delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta q\Delta q}+C_{\Delta q\Delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta g\Delta q}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\Delta q\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=g_{q\Delta q}=0\,,\phantom{\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}}

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mf_{\delta q\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5muq}\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mg_{\delta q\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=0\,,\phantom{\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}}

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mf_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{qq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{qg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+|\boldsymbol{y}|M\omega_{1}C_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mg_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=-|\boldsymbol{y}|M\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=C_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}C_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{s}

\displaystyle=g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{a}=0\,,

(353)

where we have defined

\displaystyle\omega_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\tau^{\boldsymbol{y}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{z}_{1}\!\boldsymbol{z}_{1}}}{\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{z}_{1}^{2}}=-\cos(2\varphi_{1})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\omega}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\tau^{\boldsymbol{y}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}},\boldsymbol{z}_{1}\!\boldsymbol{z}_{1}}}{\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{z}_{1}^{2}}=\sin(2\varphi_{1})

(354)

with
\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}
given in (
348
). Here
\varphi_{1}
is the angle
between
\boldsymbol{y}
and
\boldsymbol{z}_{1}
in the transverse plane, oriented such
that

\epsilon^{jj^{\prime}}_{\phantom{1}}\boldsymbol{y}^{j}_{\phantom{1}}\boldsymbol{z}^{j^{\prime}}_{1}=|\boldsymbol{y}|\mskip 1.5mu|\boldsymbol{z}_{1}|\sin\varphi_{1}
.
For mixed quark and gluon distributions the matching reads

\displaystyle f_{gq}

\displaystyle=C_{gg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gq}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}+C_{gq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qq}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\Delta g\Delta q}

\displaystyle=C_{\Delta g\Delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta g\Delta q}+C_{\Delta g\Delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta q\Delta q}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\Delta g\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=g_{g\Delta q}=0\,,\phantom{\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}-\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gq}-\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qq}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gq}-\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qq}\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mf_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{gg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+|\boldsymbol{y}|M\omega_{1}C_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t})+|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{gq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mg_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=-|\boldsymbol{y}|M\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle 2|\boldsymbol{y}|Mf_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=2|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+|\boldsymbol{y}|M\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+|\boldsymbol{y}|M\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\,,

\displaystyle 2|\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}M^{3}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t}

\displaystyle=2|\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}M^{3}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t}+|\boldsymbol{y}|M\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+|\boldsymbol{y}|M\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\,,

\displaystyle 2|\boldsymbol{y}|Mg_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}

\displaystyle=-|\boldsymbol{y}|M\tilde{\omega}_{1}(C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q})\,,

\displaystyle 2|\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}M^{3}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}^{t}

\displaystyle=-|\boldsymbol{y}|M\tilde{\omega}_{1}(C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}+C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q})

(355)

and

\displaystyle f_{qg}

\displaystyle=C_{qq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qg}+C_{qg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gg}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\Delta q\Delta g}

\displaystyle=C_{\Delta q\Delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta q\Delta g}+C_{\Delta q\Delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta g\Delta g}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\Delta q\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=g_{q\Delta g}=0\,,\phantom{\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}}

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mf_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mug}\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mg_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=0\,,\phantom{\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}C_{qq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}C_{qg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\omega_{1}C_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}g_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}|Mf_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=|\boldsymbol{y}|MC_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,,

\displaystyle|\boldsymbol{y}^{3}|M^{3}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}

\displaystyle=|\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}M^{3}C_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=g_{\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}=0\,.

(356)

For two-gluon distributions we have

\displaystyle f_{gg}

\displaystyle=C_{gg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gg}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}+C_{gq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qg}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\Delta g\Delta g}

\displaystyle=C_{\Delta g\Delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta g\Delta g}+C_{\Delta g\Delta q}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\Delta q\Delta g}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\Delta g\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=g_{g\Delta g}=0\,,\phantom{\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}-\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gg}-\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qg}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{gg}-\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{qg}\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}C_{gg}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\omega_{1}C_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}g_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}(f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t})\,,

\displaystyle 2f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}

\displaystyle=2C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,,

\displaystyle 2\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}

\displaystyle=2\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{t}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\omega_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,,

\displaystyle 2\boldsymbol{y}^{4}M^{4}g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{s}

\displaystyle=-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mug}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2}\tilde{\omega}_{1}C_{\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\underset{x_{1}}{\otimes}f_{q\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,,

\displaystyle g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{a}

\displaystyle=g_{\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}^{s}\,.

(357)

Further relations are obtained by replacing quark by antiquark labels.

Let us comment on the
\boldsymbol{y}
dependent factors in the above relations.
The factors of
|\boldsymbol{y}M|^{n}
are such that they can be completely absorbed
into the distributions (such rescaled distributions were denoted by
h
in

Diehl:2013mla
). The factors
\omega_{1}
and
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
appear
in the matching between DTMDs and DPDFs carrying different polarisations; the
azimuthal dependence they provide is required by the conservation of angular
momentum along the
z
axis. The factor
\tilde{\omega}_{1}
appears in the
matching between pseudoscalar and scalar functions and ensures that the
r.h.s. of the matching equations is odd under parity.

Appendix H
Splitting kernels for (pseudo)scalar DPDs

In this appendix, we give the matching of scalar and pseudoscalar DTMDs onto
PDFs in the region of small
\boldsymbol{y}
and
\boldsymbol{z}_{i}
. The following
results readily follow from the splitting kernels given in
section
7.4
and the tensor decompositions in
appendix
F
. The scalar matching kernels

{}^{R}T_{a_{0}\rightarrow a_{1}a_{2}}
used in the following are obtained from
those in section
7.4
by removing all tensors

\delta
,
\epsilon
and
\tau
, so that one has

{}^{1}T_{g\to q\bar{q}}=T_{F}\mskip 1.5mu(u^{2}+\bar{u}^{2})
,

{}^{1}T_{g\to\Delta q\mskip 1.5mu\bar{q}}=-iT_{F}\mskip 1.5mu(u-\bar{u})
,

{}^{1}T_{g\to\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta\bar{q}}=-2T_{F}\mskip 1.5muu\bar{u}
,

{}^{1}T_{q\to\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}=2C_{F}\mskip 1.5mu\bar{u}/u
, etc.
The only exception to this rule is that we define

{}^{1}T_{g\to\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}=2C_{A}\mskip 1.5muu\bar{u}
. Then the
coefficients
{}^{1}T_{a_{0}\to a_{1}a_{2}}
used here agree with the
coefficients
T_{a_{0}\to a_{1}a_{2}}
in appendix B of
Diehl:2014vaa
for
all channels. The kernels for other colour representations are obtained
from (
319
).

The matching between (pseudo)scalar DTMDs and PDFs then reads

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,q\bar{q}}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to q\bar{q}}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,\Delta q\Delta\bar{q}}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to\Delta q\mskip 1.5mu\Delta\bar{q}}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,\Delta q\mskip 1.5mu\bar{q}}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1}-\boldsymbol{z}_{2})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to\Delta q\mskip 1.5mu\bar{q}}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,q\mskip 1.5mu\Delta\bar{q}}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1}-\boldsymbol{z}_{2})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to q\mskip 1.5mu\Delta\bar{q}}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta\bar{q}}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to\delta q\mskip 1.5mu\delta\bar{q}}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}

(358)

for quark-antiquark DTMDs. For splitting into gluon-quark distributions we
have

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,gq}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{q\to gq}\,\frac{f_{q}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,\Delta g\Delta q}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{q\to\Delta g\Delta q}\,\frac{f_{q}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,\Delta g\mskip 1.5muq}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1}-\boldsymbol{z}_{2})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{q\to\Delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\,\frac{f_{q}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,g\mskip 1.5mu\Delta q}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1}-\boldsymbol{z}_{2})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{q\to g\mskip 1.5mu\Delta q}\,\frac{f_{q}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2})\,{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y})(\boldsymbol{y}_{-}\boldsymbol{y})-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{y}^{2}}\;\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{q\to\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\,\frac{f_{q}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2})\,{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y})(\boldsymbol{y}_{-}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}})+(\boldsymbol{y}_{-}\boldsymbol{y})(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{y}^{2}}\;\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{q\to\delta g\mskip 1.5muq}\,\frac{f_{q}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}

(359)

and for two-gluon DTMDs

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,gg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to gg}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,\Delta g\Delta g}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to\Delta g\Delta g}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,g\mskip 1.5mu\Delta g}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}}(\boldsymbol{z}_{1}-\boldsymbol{z}_{2})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to g\mskip 1.5mu\Delta g}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2})\,{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y})(\boldsymbol{y}_{-}\boldsymbol{y})-\boldsymbol{y}^{2}(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{y}^{2}}\;\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{y}^{2}M^{2})\,{}^{R}\mskip-1.5mug_{\text{spl},\,g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y})(\boldsymbol{y}_{-}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}})+(\boldsymbol{y}_{-}\boldsymbol{y})(\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\tilde{\boldsymbol{y}})}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{y}^{2}}\;\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{R}\mskip-1.5muf_{\text{spl},\,\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{z}_{i},\boldsymbol{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}\boldsymbol{y}_{-}}{\boldsymbol{y}_{+}^{2}\mskip 1.5mu\boldsymbol{y}_{-}^{2}}\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi^{2}}\,{}^{R\,}\mskip-1.5muT_{g\to\delta g\mskip 1.5mu\delta g}\,\frac{f_{g}(x_{1}+x_{2})}{x_{1}+x_{2}}\,.

(360)

Appendix I
Erratum

I.1
Colour structure of DPDs

The analysis of the colour structure of two-gluon DPDs is incomplete in the original manuscript and needs to be extended. Consider a quantity with four adjoint indices that transforms like an overall colour singlet. One can first couple the indices pairwise to one of the combinations
R=1,A,S,10,\overline{10},27
. An overall singlet is then obtained if the colour representations two index pairs are in conjugate colour representations. This includes the cases where one pair is in the antisymmetric octet and the other in the symmetric octet. These cases were omitted in
Mekhfi:1985dv
, see equations (6.a), (6.b) and (7) in that work. This mistake was repeated in equation (2.121) of
Diehl:2011yj
.

In appendix A of
Kasemets:2014yna
it was shown that the two-gluon distributions corresponding to
R=10
and
\overline{10}
in the previous construction are equal. The proof given there makes use of the colour decompositions given in
Mekhfi:1985dv
;
Diehl:2011yj
and breaks down once the missing combinations with one symmetric and one antisymmetric octet are included. We hence must extend the colour decompositions in the present work by adding the mixed octet combinations and restoring separate quantities for
R=10
and
\overline{10}
.

In the list of projection operators
P_{R}
in (
4.1
), we hence replace
P_{D}=P_{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}+P_{\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}
with the separate projectors

\displaystyle P_{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mskip 1.5mu\bigl(\delta^{ab}\delta^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}-\delta^{ab^{\prime}}\delta^{a^{\prime}b}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\,P_{A}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}-\frac{i}{4}\,\bigl(d^{abc}f^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c}+f^{abc}d^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c}\bigr)\,,

\displaystyle P_{\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mskip 1.5mu\bigl(\delta^{ab}\delta^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}-\delta^{ab^{\prime}}\delta^{a^{\prime}b}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\,P_{A}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}+\frac{i}{4}\,\bigl(d^{abc}f^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c}+f^{abc}d^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}c}\bigr)

(361)

for the decuplet and antidecuplet. These projectors agree with
P^{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}_{aa^{\prime}bb^{\prime}}
and
P^{\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}_{aa^{\prime}bb^{\prime}}
in equation (1.19) of
Keppeler:2012ih
. They also agree with the corresponding projectors in equation (12) of
Mekhfi:1985dv
, provided that we identify
\langle a^{\prime}b^{\prime}|P_{R}|ab\rangle=P_{R}^{ab\,a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}
in analogy to equation (2) of that paper. Note that the form of these projectors is correct for SU(
N
) and not restricted to
N=3

Keppeler:2012ih
.
The symmetry relation (
68
) is not valid for decuplet projectors and must be corrected to read

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}}^{\underline{s}\,\underline{r}}\,,

(362)

where
\overline{R}
is the conjugate representation of
R
. It is understood that
\overline{R}=10
for
R=\overline{10}
and that
\overline{R}=R
for the singlet, all octets, and for
R=27
. The projector relation (
69
) remains valid.

For the mixed gluon octet channels, we introduce the tensors

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}AS}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}\mskip 1.5muf^{aa^{\prime}c}d^{bb^{\prime}c}\,,

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}S\mskip-1.5muA}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{2}-4}}\mskip 1.5mud^{aa^{\prime}c}f^{bb^{\prime}c}\,,

(363)

which satisfy the relations

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}AS}^{\underline{a}\,\underline{b}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}S\mskip-1.5muA}^{\underline{b}\,\underline{a}}\,,

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}AS}^{\underline{a}\,\underline{a}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}S\mskip-1.5muA}^{\underline{a}\,\underline{a}}=0\,.

(364)

They are not projectors, since they do not satisfy (
69
).

We also note that (
69
) is incorrect for the octet tensors with mixed fundamental and adjoint indices: the contraction
P_{A}^{aa^{\prime}\,ii^{\prime}}P_{S}^{i^{\prime}i\,bb^{\prime}}
is not zero but equal to
P_{AS}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}
. The octet tensors in (
4.1
) are therefore not projectors either. Fortunately, the incorrect instances of (
69
) were not used in our calculations.

To have a uniform notation for calculations, we write the projectors as

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\,,

(365)

where the double indices
\underline{r}
and
\underline{s}
are both in the fundamental or both in the adjoint representation. We furthermore write
P_{R_{1}R_{2}\rule{0.0pt}{3.91806pt}}^{\underline{i}\,\underline{a}}=P_{R_{2}\mskip 1.5muR_{1}\rule{0.0pt}{3.91806pt}}^{\underline{a}\,\underline{i}}
for the tensors in (
4.1
), with
R_{1}=1,8
for the fundamental indices and
R_{2}=1,A,S
for the adjoint ones.
We then have the simple rules

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{2}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{2}R_{1}}^{\underline{s}\,\underline{r}}\,,

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{2}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{3}R_{4}}^{\underline{s}\,\underline{t}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{R_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}_{3}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{4}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{t}}\,.

(366)

The corrected form of (
70
) hence reads

\displaystyle P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{2}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{3}R_{4}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{R_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}_{4}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{3}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{r}}=\delta_{R_{1}\overline{R}_{3}}\,\delta_{R_{2}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}_{4}}\;m(R_{1})\,,

(367)

where the definition (
71
) of the multiplicity
m(R)
remains valid as it stands.
19
19

19

For general
N
, one obtains multiplicities
m(8)=N^{2}-1
for the octet and
m(10)=(N^{2}-1)(N^{2}-4)/4
for the decuplet representations.

The tensor
P_{R_{1}R_{2}}
couples two double-index objects in the representations
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
to an overall singlet. Hence, the two representations must have the same multiplicity. Formally one can impose this by defining
P_{RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}=0
for
m(R)\neq m(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime})
. One should then also define
P_{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}=P_{\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}=0
.

An object with four colour indices that transforms as an overall colour singlet can now be represented in terms of the tensors
P_{RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}
. The corrected form of the decomposition (
72
) reads

\displaystyle M^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}\frac{1}{m(R)}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R\mskip 1.5muR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\;\Bigl(P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}\,\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{\underline{t}\,\underline{u}}\mskip 1.5muM^{\underline{t}\,\underline{u}}_{\phantom{1}}\Bigl)\,.

(368)

A simple example illustrating the necessity to include
RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}=AS
in the sum over representation pairs is
M^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}=f^{aa^{\prime}c}\mskip 1.5mud^{bb^{\prime}c}
. The relation (
73
) correctly reads

\displaystyle M_{1}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\,M_{2}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}\frac{1}{m(R)}\,\bigl(P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R\mskip 1.5muR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\,M_{1}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\bigr)\,\bigr(P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}\,\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{\underline{t}\,\underline{u}}\,M_{2}^{\underline{t}\,\underline{u}}\bigr)\,.

(369)

Note that, for each contracted double index, the corresponding representations in
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
are conjugate to each other as a consequence of (
367
).

Various derivations and results in the paper must be adjusted to reflect the change from one to two representation labels and the conjugation of representations (which is trivial for all representations apart from the decuplet and antidecuplet). We present the necessary changes in the order in which they appear in the manuscript.

The corrected form for the colour decomposition (
74
) of a DPD is

\displaystyle F_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{\underline{r}_{1}\underline{r}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{R_{1}R_{2}}\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{a_{1}}\mathcal{N}_{a_{2}}}\,\frac{1}{\varepsilon(R_{1})\,\varepsilon(R_{2})}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{m(R_{1})}}\;{}^{R_{1}R_{2}}\mskip-1.5muF_{a_{1}a_{2}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{2}}^{\underline{r}_{1}\underline{r}_{2}}

(370)

with

\displaystyle{}^{R_{1}R_{2}}\mskip-1.5muF_{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}_{a_{1}}\mathcal{N}_{a_{2}}\,\varepsilon(R_{1})\,\varepsilon(R_{2})\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{m(R_{1})}}\;P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}_{2}}^{\underline{s}_{1}\underline{s}_{2}}F_{a_{1}a_{2}\phantom{R}}^{\underline{s}_{1}\underline{s}_{2}}\,,

(371)

where instead of
\varepsilon_{a}(R)
from (
76
) we now use

\displaystyle\varepsilon(A)

\displaystyle=i\,,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(R)

\displaystyle=1\text{\penalty\ \penalty\ for }R\neq A.

(372)

With our new notation, the definition (
79
) of colour projected twist-two operators reads

\displaystyle{}^{R}O^{\mskip 1.5mu\underline{r}}_{a\rule{0.0pt}{3.46594pt}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}_{a}\,\varepsilon(R)\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}}^{\underline{r}\underline{s}}\,O^{\mskip 1.5mu\underline{s}}_{a\rule{0.0pt}{3.46594pt}}\,,

(373)

and (
80
) must be modified to

\displaystyle 2\pi\delta(p^{+}-p^{\prime+})\,2p^{+}\,{}^{R_{1}R_{2}}\mskip-1.5muF_{\text{us},a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m(R_{1})}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu\overline{R}_{2}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\;\big\langle p^{\prime}\big|\,{}^{R_{1}}O^{\mskip 1.5mu\underline{r}}_{a_{1}}\,{}^{R_{2}}O^{\mskip 1.5mu\underline{s}}_{a_{2}}\,\big|p\big\rangle\,.

(374)

For
R_{2}=\overline{R}_{1}
, one can eliminate the projector on the r.h.s. by using that
P_{R\overline{R}}^{\underline{r}\,\underline{s}}\,{}^{R}O^{\mskip 1.5mu\underline{s}}_{a\rule{0.0pt}{3.46594pt}}={}^{R}O^{\mskip 1.5mu\underline{r}}_{a\rule{0.0pt}{3.46594pt}}
.

We modify the colour projection (
81
) of the soft matrix as

\displaystyle{}^{R_{1}R_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}S_{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon(R_{1})\,\varepsilon(R_{2})}{\varepsilon(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1})\,\varepsilon(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m(R_{1})\,m(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1})}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}_{1}\overline{R}_{2}}^{\underline{r}_{1}\underline{r}_{2}}\,S_{a_{1}a_{2}\phantom{R}}^{\underline{r}_{1}\underline{r}_{2},\underline{s}_{1}\underline{s}_{2}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}^{\underline{s}_{1}\underline{s}_{2}}\,.

(375)

The same holds for the colour space matrices
S^{-1}
,
s
,
s^{-1}
, and
K
. Together with (
369
), this leads to a modification of
the multiplication rule for matrices in representation space, which we define in general by

\displaystyle{}^{R_{1}R_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{2}}(M_{1}\cdot M_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}{}^{R_{1}R_{2},\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}M_{1}\;{}^{R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{2}}M_{2}\,,

(376)

where the projections of
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
on representations are defined as in (
375
). The correct form of (
82
) then reads

\displaystyle{}^{R_{1}R_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{2}}(S_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{-1}\cdot S_{a_{1}a_{2}})\;

\displaystyle={}^{R_{1}R_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{2}}(s_{a_{1}a_{2}}^{-1}\cdot s_{a_{1}a_{2}})\;=\delta_{R_{1}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{1}}\,\delta_{R_{2}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime\prime}_{2}}\,,

(377)

and corresponding changes are to be made in (
83
) and (
84
).
Again as a consequence of (
369
), the expression (
85
) for the cross section is modified to

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=\frac{H_{a_{1}b_{1}}}{\mathcal{N}_{a_{1}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{N}_{b_{1}}}\,\frac{H_{a_{2}b_{2}}}{\mathcal{N}_{a_{2}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathcal{N}_{b_{2}}}\,\sum_{R_{1}R_{2}}\eta(R_{1})\,\eta(R_{2})\;{}^{\overline{R}_{1}\overline{R}_{2}}\mskip-1.5muF_{b_{1}b_{2}}{}^{R_{1}R_{2}}\mskip-1.5muF_{a_{1}a_{2}}

(378)

with

\displaystyle\eta(R)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}(R)\,,

(379)

so that
\eta(A)=-1
and
\eta(R)=1
for
R\neq A
.

We now discuss the necessary adjustments to the symmetry properties derived in section
4.3
. Using that
P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}R_{1}R_{2}}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}=\bigl[P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}\overline{R}_{1}\overline{R}_{2}}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}\mskip 1.5mu\bigr]^{*}
, one finds that (
88
) and (
89
) must be corrected to

\displaystyle{}^{R_{1}R_{2},\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}S_{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left({}^{R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2},\overline{R}_{1}\overline{R}_{2}}S_{a_{1}a_{2}}\right)^{*}\,,

(380)

\displaystyle{}^{R_{1}R_{2},\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}S_{a_{1}a_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1})\,\eta(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2})}{\eta(R_{1})\,\eta(R_{2})}\,\left({}^{\overline{R}_{1}\overline{R}_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}S_{\bar{a}_{1}\bar{a}_{2}}\right)^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1})\,\eta(R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2})}{\eta(R_{1})\,\eta(R_{2})}\;{}^{\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}\overline{R}{}^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2},R_{1}R_{2}}S_{\bar{a}_{1}\bar{a}_{2}}\,.

(381)

An analogous correction is to be made for (
90
). The result that the soft matrix in representation space is real valued remains true, so that the complex conjugation in (
380
) and (
I.1
) can be omitted. Doing this in the first line of (
I.1
), one finds that

\displaystyle{}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$},\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}S_{gg}

\displaystyle={}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$},\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}S_{gg}\,,

\displaystyle{}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$},\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}S_{gg}

\displaystyle={}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$},\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}S_{gg}\,,

(382)

which is expected because charge conjugation interchanges the representations
10
and
\overline{10}
. Furthermore, one finds that
{}^{R_{1}R_{2},R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{1}R^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}_{2}}S_{gg}=0
if there is an odd number of representations
A
and no representation is equal to
10
or
\overline{10}
. This reflects the fact that the antisymmetric and the symmetric gluon octets transform under charge conjugation with a relative minus sign. Corresponding results hold for the Collins-Soper Kernel
K_{gg}
.

Using that
\varepsilon(R_{1})\,\varepsilon(R_{2})\mskip 1.5muP_{\rule{0.0pt}{3.91806pt}\overline{R}_{1}\overline{R}_{2}}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}=\bigl[\mskip 1.5mu\varepsilon(R_{1})\,\varepsilon(R_{2})\mskip 1.5muP_{\rule{0.0pt}{3.91806pt}R_{1}R_{2}}^{a^{\prime}\!a\;b^{\prime}\mskip-1.5mub}\,\bigr]^{*}
, one finds that
{}^{R_{1}R_{2}}\mskip-1.5muF_{a_{1}a_{2}}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{y})
is real valued in all colour channels except for the decuplets, for which one has
{}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}\mskip 1.5mu\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}}\mskip-1.5muF_{gg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{y})=\bigl[\mskip 1.5mu{}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$\overline{10}$}\,\scalebox{0.7}{$10\phantom{\overline{1}}\hskip-3.01389pt$}}\mskip-1.5muF_{gg}(x_{i},\boldsymbol{y})\bigr]^{*}
.

The soft factor for collinear DPDs.

The relations (
100
) and (
101
) become

\displaystyle W^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{ab}\,W^{\dagger}_{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{bb^{\prime}\,cc^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{aa^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}\,W^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{bc}\,W^{\dagger}_{c^{\prime}b^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle W^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{ab}\,W^{\dagger}_{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{bb^{\prime}\,k^{\prime}k}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{aa^{\prime}\,j^{\prime}j}\,W^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{jk}\,W^{\dagger}_{k^{\prime}j^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle W^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{ij}\,W^{\dagger}_{j^{\prime}i^{\prime}}\,P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{jj^{\prime}\,aa^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=P_{\rule{0.0pt}{4.82224pt}RR^{\mskip 1.5mu\prime}}^{ii^{\prime}\,bb^{\prime}}\,W^{\phantom{\dagger}}_{ab}\,W^{\dagger}_{b^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\,,

(383)

so that (
102
) generalises to
